## What the sentences actually asked for

I read all 23 instruction and 21 permission sentences before researching anything. Neither is one thing.

**Instruction splits four ways.** Six say *attend* ("Notice what arrives before the explanation", "Feel the field"). Four say *act* ("Check it", "Make the appointment", "Do the work"). One redirects the question. And **ten â€” the largest group by far â€” are prohibitions**: "Do not grab it", "Do not decorate it", "Do not turn it immediately into a story", "Do not rush to tell everyone".

That group had no name, and the gap is in the project's own rule rather than its research. H10 lists the three jobs a negative can do â€” lifts a requirement, corrects a reading, denies a loss â€” and gives each a hand. None of them is *forbids an action the listener was about to take*. I checked every neighbouring concept and all five fail; `letting go` is the exact inverse ("a requirement lifted off the listener"), so filing "Do not grab it" there would put a releasing hand on a line that imposes. So I added the concept **refraining**.

**Permission splits four ways too.** Eleven are *let it* ("Just let it watch", "So let that part learn gently"), five are collective and already in the air ("permission is collective"), two are the leave refused, and one lowers a gate of her own.

A second orphan turned up while checking. **`protect_small_flame` was already in the base** â€” written for this lesson's own line about carrying a first flame into a room full of wind â€” and reachable only under `smallness`, `boundary` and `reassurance`, because no concept contained the word protect. Seventh recorded instance of this project's standing fault. So **protecting** ships too, deliberately the smallest concept here at five sentences, and it sits exactly where the two shelves meet: "Protect them" is an order, "Let it grow where it is safe" is leave, and both are one act.

## What I could not fill, and why

The ban is real and it costs the largest single piece of permission. This is measured, not assumed: the Swedish lexicon's own entry for *to allow* is thumb-hands moving forward while **twisting upward** into flat hands, and its entry for *may / be allowed* is flat hands upward-directed and struck forward. Kendon names what both belong to â€” the Open Hand Supine family, theme "offering, giving or showing something or requesting the reception of something". The whole supine route is closed.

What is genuinely lost is **the welcome** â€” permission offered warmly to a person present. There is no compliant substitute and I recorded it as a hole rather than approximating it. The four permission gestures are built from the only logic left: a block that stands down, a grip let go without the hand leaving, hands that come down and stop rather than shape, and a leave held between two closed hands.

Four fetched families were rejected outright and the rejections shaped the pack. **VAKTA**, the sign for guarding, is the double-hook in both variants â€” a two-finger shape, banned â€” so nothing here depicts keeping watch, the same wall the audience pack hit with the sign for looking. NORMAL is a V-hand at the nose twisting up. BEFALLA, for commanding, starts at the mouth and is thrown forward. The lexicon's "don't touch" is the middle finger, repeated.

## Sources fetched

Kendon's *Gesture: Visible Action as Utterance* ch. 13 via Cambridge Core, whose Open Hand Prone family ("denied, negated, interrupted or stopped") supplies every prohibition entry. Harrison's negation-timing paper, read from the PDF, which measures that speakers synchronise the stroke with the negative and then **hold through its whole scope** â€” independently the single-motion-ending-in-a-hold this base already demands for anti-loop reasons. And twenty-one Swedish Sign Language Lexicon entries.

## A fault worth knowing

Three of my ten entries first built with a link to `letting go` â€” the one shelf refraining exists to avoid â€” and **in every case the link came from the entry's own warning against it**. The builder reads `use_when` as linking text and cannot see the word "Never", so "Never on a negative that LIFTS a requirement" contains the letting-go keyword verbatim. I reworded all three. Auditing the base, **23 of about 600 secondary links have this defect** â€” entries filed on shelves they explicitly exclude. I left it alone (fixing it means rewording existing entries) and flagged it as a separate task.

Build exits 0; guard reports nothing inert, all 296 offerable gestures reachable. Every new gesture passes the hard-rule, cycle, render-risk and adjacency screens â€” one H3 violation was caught by the project's screen and fixed, not by my judgement.

Cue widenings, each read in full: instruction gained six sentences by allowing a discourse marker before the imperative ("Just notice it", "Protect them", "First, listen") â€” three of those previously matched nothing. Permission gained exactly one, "reality is *permitted* to speak in only one way", which is the second half of the pair the standing-bar gesture was written for. The refraining cue's first draft took a wrong sentence, "You do not become telepathic by force" â€” a statement, not an order â€” and requiring the imperative form removed it at no cost.

Two files changed: `gesture_kb/build_gesture_kb.py` and the new `gesture_catalog/gesture_library_research_v12.json`.

One question. The ten prohibitions are the biggest single block in this lesson, and three of them (spot 12's "Do not trust what you cannot prove", "Do not become strange") are the *culture* speaking, not Anna â€” I let both land on the refraining shelf and told the worker to read the line. ## 1. The self-excluded links â€” true count: **27**, not 23

Measured by rebuilding, not estimated. Secondary links before: **600** (1344 total). After: **573** (1317 total). Exactly **27 removed, 0 added** â€” I compared the full link sets, not just the counts. The 27 come from **20 entries spread across all 8 library packs**, so this was never a wording habit in one pack; it was the builder.

### The fix is builder-side

`C:\moma_forks\prompter2\sc10\combo_runner\gesture_kb\build_gesture_kb.py` now blanks a *forbidding clause* before anything is matched. A clause counts as forbidding when it is headed by "never" or "not" immediately followed by a situational word â€” where, when, on, for, in, as, with, after, before, while, if â€” at the start of a sentence or a co-ordinate clause; or when the sentence carries a verdict on use ("must be avoided", "must not be used", "do not use it that way").

Two deliberate narrowings, both measured:

- **The situational word is required.** Without it, honest motion text stops linking â€” "the hand never lifts", "which never stands on an edge", "never a counting one". A first draft that also admitted "not to" reached into ordinary contrast clauses ("belongs to the listener, not to her") and began masking real meaning.
- **"Do not â€¦" is NOT treated generally** â€” only three fixed phrases are. The refraining entries *quote* the lesson's own prohibitions ("Do not fight it", "Do not grab it", "Do not decorate it") and those quotations are exactly what those entries mean. A general rule would have blinded the base to its own newest shelf.

Masking is **per occurrence, not per entry** â€” the crucial difference from rewording by hand.

## 2. The quoted prohibitions â€” implemented as you decided

The three culture-spoken lines are spot 12's: "Do not trust what you cannot prove", "Do not become strange", "Do not cross the invisible wall between one mind and another". The line cues are **untouched** â€” those lines stay on `refraining`.

The bar entry also says the forbidding is real either way, so the line still belongs to the shelf, and that the worker chooses by reading who is speaking.

The build now prints those three suppressions by name, so the choice is auditable rather than clever.

I also corrected the v12 pack's own note, which was teaching the next author to contort wording around keywords. That workaround is now obsolete and the note says so.

## The true extent â€” measured, not estimated

I added a link audit to the builder (`--link-audit`) that records, for every meaning link, **which field of the entry created it and the exact sentence**. That turned the question into a count.

An entry has five fields. Three state meaning â€” `meaning`, `semantic_tags`, `use_when` ("the line ..."). Two describe the hands â€” `name` ("Quiet trace along the table edge") and `description` ("her fingers relaxed and slightly apart"). Both were being mined for concept keywords.

**352 of 1313 links â€” 27% of the whole base â€” existed only because of a word in the two motion fields.** 284 from `description`, 68 from `name`. Far more than the four examples. The full list, every one with its causing sentence, is regenerated on every build to `motion_link_refusals_v01.txt` in the gesture_kb folder.

The worst offenders, with the word that made them:

| meaning | wrong links | the word |
|---|---|---|
| something arriving | 38 | "comes to" Ã—31 â€” the house ending, *comes to rest* |
| grounding | 32 | "surface", "flat on the table" â€” the table |
| separation | 25 | "apart" â€” fingers slightly apart |
| opening | 20 | "open" â€” including gestures that then shut |
| stillness | 20 | "unhurried", "quiet" â€” how the motion is performed |
| boundary | 19 | "edge" â€” the table edge, the little-finger edge |
| time passing | 19 | "before" â€” "...*before* lowering again" |
| sincerity | 13 | "chest" â€” chest **height** |

The clearest proof it was not over-culling: the **expansion** shelf held 13 gestures. Eleven were entries whose fingers merely start *spread* â€” several of which then close ("spread hand draws down and shuts", "spread hands close in and settle"). A worker asking for expansion was being handed contraction.

## What I changed, and why that route

**Structural, not a blocklist.** A meaning link may now read only the fields authored to state meaning. `name` and `description` are no longer linking text at all. Every earlier fix in this family was a per-word patch and each left the next word standing; this states the rule once and cannot rot.

Two consequences I also fixed:
- The house-style filter measured a corpus the linker no longer reads, so it was recomputed over `use_when` alone. Its blocklist shrank from 14 keywords to 3 â€” "rest", "then", "still" were universal in *motion* prose, and in a use_when they discriminate again. This gained back ~45 genuine links.
- The `hearing` / "voice" instance **survives the structural fix**, because it lives in properly authored meaning text: every `quoted voice` entry says "quoted voice" in its own tags, and `hearing` carried the bare stem "voice". All 9 links that keyword made were quoted-voice or permission entries; not one was about sound. That is a **second family** â€” a keyword that spells another meaning's *name*. I removed the bare "voice" and added a build scan that names the whole class (7 more: "weigh"/weight, "stop"/stopping, "releas"/energy release, "look"/looking around, "self"/self satisfaction, "quiet"/quieting, "present"/presenting). Those seven are **reported, not blocked** â€” most are honest synonyms and culling them would delete real signal.

**Fifteen entries repaired in the database, not the matcher.** About 15 of the 352 were true claims that only the motion text happened to carry â€” a name here is sometimes a caption plus a meaning gloss ("Two sides weighed unevenly", "Hand tilts in doubt", "leaving something unsaid"). Splitting a name at its comma is the keyword cleverness the rule exists to end, so I corrected their `semantic_tags` instead. Five of those were gestures that lost their *last* link â€” exactly your predicted case, reachable only by a wrong link â€” including one whose tag already said "letting go" and could never match, because the keyword is "let go" and a word boundary will not find it inside "letting".

## The five spared links â€” all five fall

Every one was kept last pass because a geometry word appeared outside the exclusion clause, and every one of those second mentions was motion text: *"its little-finger EDGE on the table"*, *"comes to a HALT early"*, *"touches her own CHEST"*, *"side by side"*, *"apart"*. So that reasoning was the defect itself.

- protect_hand_stands_betweenâ€¦ â†’ boundary â€” **gone**
- still_hand_stops_earlyâ€¦ â†’ stopping â€” **gone**
- watched_closed_hand_touches_her_own_chestâ€¦ â†’ sincerity â€” **gone**
- watched_spread_hands_close_inâ€¦ â†’ comparison â€” **gone**
- watched_spread_hands_close_inâ€¦ â†’ separation â€” **gone**

All four gestures remain reachable by other meanings. Pinned by name in the self-test so this cannot be quietly re-undone.

## What the sentences actually asked for

I read all 385 narration sentences through the renderer's own loader before researching anything.

**Opening â€” 26 sentences, and they are four different acts, not one.** Five are *the door as a standing object* ("The problem is not that the door is locked", "The door was still there, but nobody was looking at it") â€” the lesson's opening image and its central one, and the base depicted no door at all. Seven are *a person becoming open*, which is a settled state and not an event ("You become available", "Availability", "Grounded and open"); spot 32 defines the word itself â€” "You no longer say no before you have heard what is being said. You become available." Five are *an opening underway and noticed rather than performed* ("Now it is beginning to open", "Ahâ€¦ something is opening"). Five are spot 19's run of conditions ("It opens in love. It opens in play. It opens when nobody is trying to win"). Two are cue noise.

**Refraining â€” 13 sentences, and the hole was not thinness but refusal.** Four of the thirteen are spot 12, the culture's own prohibitions, and **all three existing entries name spot 12 in their own text and say never here**, because each lays the bar with Anna's own hand. The fallback they point at is the quoted-voice family, which places a *voice speaking* â€” but spot 12 is not somebody talking, it is a rule she has lived inside. A second, smaller gap: twice the lesson forbids a *starting place* rather than an act and names a better one immediately ("do not begin by askingâ€¦" then "Ask insteadâ€¦"), and nothing on the shelf refuses a point of departure. The rest of the shelf is genuinely covered and I wrote nothing for it.

## Layout: I refused to fill it, and that is the first result

Layout looks worst of the three (21 sentences, 5 options) and is **not a supply problem**. The project's own instrument already knew: its windows measure a mean of **1.08 seconds across thirteen of them against 2.18 seconds for all 195 windows in the lesson**, and two are literally zero seconds long. Reading the sentences says the same thing â€” fifteen of the twenty-one are bare list items ("Health." "Work." "Love." "Through people."), and H11 already rules that a list is one gesture placed *before* it while the items stay still. Real demand is four or five list heads against five entries, one written for exactly that slot. Filling it would have been padding a shelf whose problem is measured in seconds.

I did find a genuine instrument fault there and deliberately left it: the layout cue catches the *items* and misses most of the *heads*, so the one place a laying-out motion legitimately belongs is the one place no sentence can ask for it. Every repair I drafted was a pattern written against two specific sentences â€” the overfitting the previous pass exists to stop, and a cue that can see a list head would have to see the *next* sentence, which a per-sentence pattern cannot.

## Also recorded as unfillable, not reopened

Spot 19's five "It opens inâ€¦" clauses are the effortlessness cluster the previous pass already recorded as unfillable, because every documented manual form is a supinated open palm or a shrug. ## What I filled â€” five entries, no new vocabulary

Three opening: **the way through stands clear** (two hands standing edge-on, shut against each other, one travels clear and stays open); **it has started to open and gone no further** (the fingertips of a flat hand lift off the wood while the heel stays down, and stay lifted); **the refusal is no longer being made** (a hand standing across lowers and lies down â€” availability as a refusal withdrawn, not a gift). Two refraining: **a rule that was already lying there** (the index sets somebody else's rule down away from her centre and leaves it); **not from here** (a closed hand comes down on the very spot a journey would have set off from and occupies it).

Every one links through concepts that already exist and already carry cues, so **I wrote and widened no cue at all** and the unmatched count is untouched. That was deliberate: two of the three inertness traps are a gesture the vocabulary cannot name and a meaning no sentence can ask for, and aiming at shelves that already have both avoids them by construction. The pack is registered in the library list in the same edit as the file itself.

## Bans checked before writing, not after

The obvious opening form â€” hands parting palm-up â€” is banned outright, so nothing here turns a palm over; the door hands stand on their little-finger edges with the flats facing each other, which keeps them clear of both the palm-up ban and the palm-toward-viewer watch. One of the availability entries lands flat on the table, permitted only because what the hand becomes is a resting hand, with "without pressing" keeping it off the hard press.

## Sources fetched, and two rejections that shaped the pack

Swedish Sign Language Lexicon, Stockholm University: Ã–PPNA 00292, Ã–PPEN 15301, DÃ–RR 11721, TILLGÃ„NGLIG 08616, REGEL 01169, BÃ–RJA 02945, VÃ„GRA 03973.

TILLGÃ„NGLIG was kept only for its placement finding (Swedish makes availability *on the body*, so it is a property of the person, not a gap in front of her) while its thumb-hand form was dropped as a thumbs-up at candlelight. A rule and a beginning are the same bodily event pointed opposite ways, which is why both refraining entries come out of one source, and it decided the form for the opening-barely-underway entry too.

- **One inverts outright.** "So we're not going to fire the guard" is a removal *declined*; the actual setting-aside line in that spot is the next one.

**Affection is not romantic and barely metaphorical.** Seven of its 21 sentences are somebody *needing* somebody â€” a mother and a child in distress, a child who cannot be heard in ordinary language, a friend who needs comfort â€” and the most exact statement of the act is spot 52: "Someone who loves you can send just by holding you in their awareness." That is warmth toward a person **who is not in the room**, and the seven existing entries had no route to it. Six sentences across spots 14, 15, 22, 47 and 52 ask for it, five of those spots are still undesigned, and the windows are ordinary at 2.08 seconds.

## What I filled

Three entries, one source, one per verb. Delsarte's law of retroaction, fetched from the Gutenberg text: *"The hand must tend toward the loved being to caress, to grasp, to reassure or to defend."* The caress that runs out of table; the grasp taken down to a keeping; the reassurance that gets there and gives the person a seat. The fourth verb, to defend, is sheltering and belongs to `protecting`, so I left it.

The bans were checked first, and the source solved them rather than a workaround did. Delsarte forbids the forward lean himself â€” *"A being who feels, draws back, and contemplates the object toward which the hand extends"* â€” so love in his system is a hand going out while the trunk goes back. That is why all three put the loved person out on the **table** and never at the viewer, which is correct here because the beloved is always a third party. No chest, no face, no offered palm.

**A warning I recorded and did not act on:** the same passage calls the hand carried to the heart in this sentiment "an oratorical crime", and two entries already on the shelf do exactly that. Nothing retired, nothing touched â€” the next session should just know the classical authority is against them.

## What I rejected, from sources I fetched

The Swedish Sign Language Lexicon's entire care-about family is **one sign wearing several glosses** â€” bry sig om, ta hand om, bevara â€” angle hands pointing upward, contact at the **mouth**, then forward in an arc. That is H1's reach plus a hand over the mouth of a woman who is mid-sentence. Comfort draws both hands down the two cheeks alternately (H4 and H14) and is the only documented Swedish form for comforting anyone. Long-for is palms up and repeated; tend sweeps forward-facing open palms side to side. The pattern is the finding worth keeping: Swedish makes caring with the hands open and up, because caring there is *offering* care, and this vocabulary cannot say offering at all.

- **The enlargement act** â€” *it is that, and more than that* â€” has no concept and no gesture anywhere. A cue alone would have aimed sentences at an empty shelf, which is trap three rather than a fix.

"The card is not asking for help." â€” plus "The message has no life in it.", which is one of only two sentences in all 385 matching nothing. All four sit in spots 16 and 17, both undesigned, and every one that matches anything matches **only** affection. A warmth gesture on any of them says the opposite of the line. That needs a concept, a cue and a form together.

# `expansion`: refuse the fill

## 1. What the demanding sentences actually ask for

The demand report gives `expansion` **script 2, realised 0, supply 3** â€” and marks it *"no line cue â€” script figure unreliable"*. It has no sentence-side cue at all, so that 2 comes from the gesture-side keywords `expand / wider / spread / grow` fired at narration. `--why expansion` refuses to explain it for exactly that reason.

So I read all 385 sentences myself rather than trust the 2. The keyword net's two catches are:

- spot 24 (undesigned): *"Let it grow where it is safe."*
- spot 44 (designed): *"And slowly, trust grows."*

**Both collapse on reading, and they collapse in different directions â€” which is the finding.**

Spot 24 sits inside *"Do not take the first small flame into a room full of wind. / Keep it quiet. / Let it grow where it is safe."* The growing is what the flame does; what **she** does is shelter it and leave it alone. A widening motion there contradicts *"Keep it quiet"* two sentences earlier. That line belongs to `protecting` (2 offerable â€” thinner than expansion) and `letting go` (26). It is the H10 error again: labelled by its noun, not by its act.

Spot 44 is the last beat of an accumulation ramp â€” *"First there's a little flicker. / Then maybe it happens again. / Then you start to recognize it. / And slowly, trust grows."* The act is recognition through repetition, not widening. And one more that is neither:

- spot 76 (designed): *"Experiences that had seemed rare began happening constantly."* â€” frequency increasing, not size or scope.

**So `expansion` is three acts wearing one name:**

**(A) a quantity accumulating over time** â€” spots 24, 44. Temporal, small, sheltered. Belongs to `progression` / `protecting` / `letting go`.
**(B) a scope widening outward to take in more** â€” spots 37, 82. This is the only one the shelf was built for.
**(C) a thing receding out of reach** â€” no sentence asks for it, yet it occupies a third of the shelf. See Â§3.

| *"Let it grow where it is safe."* | 24 | 2.1 s |

Spot 37 is **the single longest window in the entire lesson**. The two genuine act-B lines average **7.3 s against 2.40 s** â€” three times the lesson average. This is the exact inverse of `layout`, which was refused at 1.08 s. Timing is not the blocker and cannot be used to refuse this shelf.

- **`distance_hand_recedes`** â€” **inverted, and it is the same bug the link audit was built to kill.** Its use_when is *"something is receding, departing, or slipping out of view."* It reaches the expansion shelf on one word: its meaning text says *"growing distant"*, and `grow` is an expansion keyword. ## 4. What I fetched â€” and what I rejected

**Delsarte, *System of Oratory*, Chapter VII "Of The Limbs", section "Of the Hand"** (verified verbatim at fulltextarchive.com; the Gutenberg 12200 page truncates before this section):

**Swedish sign lexicon (Stockholm University), all pages fetched:**

**Italian** (LearnAmo's inventory of 63 gestures, descended from de Jorio via Munari): the gesture for *tanto / molto* is performed **"with your hand open, starting from a low position and moving it upward with decisive movement, or spreading your arms as wide as possible."** **Rejected on three counts:** an open hand lifted, arms leaving the table, and "decisive" is the opposite of her register.

**de Jorio, *La mimica degli antichi* (1832)** â€” three Archive.org scans fetched. All three return front matter, the introduction and the *AbbiccÃ¬ de' Gesti* only; the alphabetical dictionary is past the text the fetcher returns, so I could not reach entries for *abbondanza*, *molto*, *grandezza* or *crescere*. **Recorded as not reached, not as absent.**

The pattern across all of it repeats the finding earlier passes made about offering and about "nothing": **every culture makes expansion with the palms opening upward and outward, and this vocabulary cannot say that.**

**The one candidate that survived every ban** â€” offered as held in reserve, not proposed:

**SPRIDA / spread, Swedish lexicon 01882** â€” *"O-hÃ¤nder, framÃ¥triktade och nedÃ¥tvÃ¤nda, kontakt med varandra, fÃ¶rs framÃ¥t och frÃ¥n varandra samtidigt som de fÃ¶rÃ¤ndras till sprethÃ¤nder."* Two O-hands (fingertips gathered to a point) touching each other, moving forward and apart while opening into spread hands. It would need scaling to Anna's register â€” barely above the tabletop, ending well short of the near edge, with the trunk staying back per Delsarte's law of Retroaction (*"A being who feels, draws back, and contemplates the object toward which the hand extends"*), so that "forward" never becomes reaching at the camera.

## The true count

**56 of 283 secondary links â€” 20% â€” existed only because of a word inside a quoted lesson sentence.** Not 62, not 55. I could not reproduce the 435 figure either; the base as built holds 330 gestures and 1063 links, of which 283 are secondary. The one encouraging thing is that the earlier estimate of "55 remaining" and my measured 56 land in the same place from different directions.

## How I identified quoted material, and why that route

By actual quotation marks â€” but only after checking that they are a real convention rather than my assumption. The base quotes with single quotes: 163 quoted spans in `use_when` against 6 double-quote characters in the whole library. Two details had to be right or the measurement lies:

- An apostrophe with a letter on both sides (`don't`, `person's`) is inside a word, not a quote mark. My first detector got this wrong and under-counted by ten links, because the lesson's sentences are full of contractions.
- A quotation closes on a mark of its own family, so when the lesson quotes somebody inside a line the author quotes â€” `'So do not begin by asking, "Can I prove telepathy?"'` â€” the inner question stays masked instead of being left exposed.

I did **not** move quotes to the note field. That is a data edit that fixes today's entries and prevents nothing. The rule is structural: quoted text stops being linking text. `use_when` is stored and printed whole, so the worker and any human reader still see the quoted line â€” only the matcher goes blind to it, exactly as the existing self-exclusion rule blinds it to "never whereâ€¦" clauses.

**Scope was measured, not assumed.** I intended to apply this everywhere, as the self-exclusion rule is applied. Counting spans field by field stopped me: `use_when` has 163 and every one is a lesson sentence; `meaning` has exactly three, and all three are a different construction â€” "A gentle 'no'", "'I mean this'", "A soft 'hold on'". Those are the gesture speaking, not a specimen of anything external, and they are the meaning itself. Widening the rule would have cost `gentle_boundary_two_hands` its `stopping` link, which it genuinely has and holds nowhere else. So the rule is scoped to `use_when`, and the reason is written into the code so nobody "unifies" it later and quietly loses that.

## What falls

All 56 are printed at the end of every build with the exact word and the exact quotation, and written to `quoted_link_refusals_v01.txt`. The shape of it:

- **26 are the pronoun "you"** inside second-person narration. Lesson 1 addresses the listener throughout, so almost every quoted line contains it. That put gestures meaning affection, permission, sending, refusing and demanding proof onto the `addressing you` shelf.
- **5 `time passing`** on "before" â€” "â€¦before they appear in your life".
- **3 `looking around`** on "world"; **3 `acceptance`** on "allow"; **2 each** on "little", "even", "open", "comfort"; the rest singles.

**Three filed an entry under the opposite of what it means**, which is the clearest proof a quotation is not a claim:

- `light_both_hands_rise_a_little_and_carry_nothing` means lightness â€” "what she is speaking of is unweighted" â€” and sat on **weight**, because its example line is *'And the guard is very, very heavy.'*
- `still_hands_keep_their_shape_through_the_pause` means a held pause and sat on **something arriving**, on *'it never actually arrives.'*
- `proof_closed_hand_settles_and_the_proving_becomes_a_weight` means a standing demand for proof and sat on **acceptance**, on *'We want certainty before we allow experience'*.

## What survived, and why

Masking is per-occurrence, not per-entry. **Four links are named inside a quotation and earned again outside it, and all four are kept** â€” for instance `instruct_clawed_fingertips_come_down_and_mark_where_it_appeared` keeps `something arriving` on the author's own sentence "tells the listener to catch something at the moment it arrives", quite apart from the line it quotes. Whole-entry suppression would have thrown those away.

Nothing was stranded. **No gesture lost its last link**, so nothing fell into the stillness fallback and no meaning was left with zero offerable gestures â€” so I had no wrong link to replace with a right one.

## The endorsing test

The check was called *"LINKING a quoted 'Do notâ€¦' still links"*, and its stated reason was that "the refraining entries QUOTE the lesson's own prohibitions, and those quotations are exactly what they mean". That reason is precisely the rule this pass overturns.

It was also not testing what its name said. Measured: the assertion was carried by the words **"forbids"** and **"the act is withheld"**, both in the author's own half of the sentence. The quoted fragment alone makes no refraining link and never did, under the old pipeline or the new one. So it passed while proving something other than its title â€” which is worse than a failing test, because it reads as coverage.

I replaced it with a check that keeps the thing genuinely worth protecting â€” the refraining shelf must not be blinded by a rule aimed at quotations â€” and pins the true reason it survives, asserting both halves: the full sentence links, the quoted fragment alone does not. The replacement carries the argument in the test itself.

I also found the test helpers were re-implementing the masking instead of calling the builder's, so they were exercising a pipeline the builder does not have. They now route through the builder's own per-field masking, stated once in the code.

Nine new checks were added: the core rule in both directions, the per-occurrence survival case, a contraction control, a nested-quotation control, the measured `meaning`-field exemption, and three live checks against the built base including the named inversions. **Negative control run:** with the rule disabled, three of them fail, including the live inversion check listing all five pairs back on their wrong shelves. They are not vacuous.

## Measured before and after

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| links total | 1063 | **1007** (56 removed, 0 gained) |
| primary | 774 | **774** (untouched) |
| secondary (audited) | 283 | **227** |
| offerable gestures reachable | 310 of 310 | **310 of 310** |
| offerable parked in stillness fallback | 0 | **0** |
| meanings with no offerable gesture | 0 | **0** |
| `prompter2 --self-test` | 170 pass | **179 pass, 0 fail** |
| demand report unmatched | â€” | **1 of 385** |

Build exits 0; the inertness guard reports nothing inert. The inertness guard, the motion-text rule and the negated-clause rule were not touched â€” the diff deletes four lines in the build script, all of them rewiring, none of them a rule.

## Found and not fixed

- **`addressing you` is now the thinnest shelf against real demand**: 18 offerable gestures for 83 script sentences. The 26 removed were genuinely wrong â€” the survivors all say "listener" in their own meaning â€” but this shelf is now the honest first target for a research pass, and it is the highest-demand meaning in the lesson.
- **A test can pass while proving something other than its name.** I found one instance and fixed it; I did not audit the remaining 170 checks for that pattern. It is worth a pass of its own.
- **The seven cross-named keywords** are still reported and deliberately not blocked. Unchanged, pre-existing.
- If anyone ever widens this rule to the `meaning` field, `gentle_boundary_two_hands` must have its `stopping` meaning restated in its tags first. That is written into the code beside the rule.

No job was fired, no money spent, nothing written outside the `prompter2` fork, and nothing committed.</result>

# TLDR

The repetition gap was **real**. The screen guarding the finished prose had no repetition rule at all â€” nine plainly repetitive sentences went through it clean. I wired it to the vocabulary screen's own rule (not a copy) plus the cycle scan, with a mask so Max's own words are never read as motion instructions. Run over the whole stored history it flags **2 prompts that were actually fired**, and on one of them Max's own comment is a repetition complaint. Everything else in the residue is repaired and measured. Nothing fired, nothing committed, no money spent.

## The screen map I established

Four screens, not two:

1. **The vocabulary screen** (`screen_motion` â†’ `screen_prompt`, in the builder). Reads a gesture entry's **motion text only**. Enforces H1 palm-up, H2 two-finger/V, H3 left-hand gesture, H4 eye-zone touch, H5 interlaced rest, H7 repetition. Exemptions: a negated clause, a repetition word inside a budget ("at most twice in the whole lesson"), a left hand described by shape.
2. **The stored-prompt screen** â€” the *same* function, run at build time over prompts already fired, with reviewed per-prompt overrides. It **labels** history; it is not a gate.
3. **The cycle scan** (`scan_cycle`) â€” report-only, over motion text. Catches a there-and-back; deliberately does **not** treat a return to rest as a cycle.
4. **The firing gate** (`screen_composed_prompt`) â€” the only screen over the prose actually sent to the renderer. It ran H1, H2, H5, H6 left-hand, H12, camera moves, the banned-phrase family, H4 and two H14 rules. **It ran no repetition rule and no cycle scan.**

## The gap was real, and the cycle half was the bigger hole

The H7-shaped hole leaked **one** prompt. The cycle-shaped hole leaked **nineteen**. An H7-only fix would have closed the small one and left the large one open, so the gate now runs both â€” calling the builder's `screen_hits(only=("H7",))` and `cycle_hits`, never a second copy. There is a test that repoints the builder's regexes and proves the gate's verdict follows.

**The trap I had to avoid:** Max's own narration contains the exact words. Spot 3 says "Again and again." and spot 89 says "you may move back and forth." Screened raw, the gate would refuse two spots of Lesson 1 for words Max wrote. The gate now blanks the quoted lesson sentence before these two checks â€” and only these two; every other rule is untouched. Finding the quoted span needed care: 13 of the 112 spots contain a quote mark *inside* the narration.

## What the new gate finds across the stored prompt history

Nothing was changed and nothing fired â€” this is a read-only report.

- **57 Prompter-2 designs â†’ 19 flagged.** All from two rest-rotation sentences ("palms openâ€¦ then close softly *again*" Ã—14, "then part *again*" Ã—5). Both already fixed in the library; these are historical designs.
- **76 fired prompts â†’ 2 flagged.** Job 3616, "taps the table **twice**". And job 3663, "tips forward **once more** in a smaller, gentler echo of the first" â€” whose Max comment reads: *"one point of the speaker at least once is sufficient. Doing two times and holding is too strongâ€¦ it should be one touch and then kind of come back to balance not overdo it."* The gate would have caught that one before it was sent.
- **331 gesture descriptions â†’ 1 verdict moved**, a banned tombstone entry that is never offered.
- **Zero verdict changes anywhere that the new rule does not explain.**

## Repairs, each with before/after

| | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| `single_reassuring_pat` | "**pats** her other hand once" | "settles once onto her other hand" â€” library cycle scan now reports clean |
| `dissolving` definition | "â€¦or a world growing **denser** around it" | rewritten; dead `denser` keyword removed â€” **zero** link change, build output byte-identical |
| H3 comma window | "Her left hand rests**,** then lifts once." passed | flagged; guarded against "right"/"other" so a resting left hand beside a moving right hand stays clean |
| `contraction` shelf | 10 links | 7 â€” removed a creature that *is* small, an instruction to ask for less, and one that disclaims the shelf in its own sentence |
| three inversions | acceptance / progression / closing | removed; each was a word inside a clause denying it |

`contraction`: I kept two and say why â€” narrowing a claim *is* narrowing (and "narrow" is the concept's own word), and condensing a long matter *is* condensing.

Links 1005 â†’ 999, exactly the six intended. Gestures 331, meanings 73, **311 offerable all reachable, nothing inert.**

## Negative controls â€” every new check proved sensitive

Builder's regexes repointed â†’ the gate's verdict moves with them. H3 comma window removed â†’ the two comma cases fail. # TLDR

The gate can now judge H8, and the acceptance test passes: the beat Max refused on spot 88 is caught, the beat he praised on job 3609 passes. But the headline finding is a correction â€” **the wording can never separate those two beats, because they are the same shape.** Only the meaning separates them, so Task B alone could never have worked.

## Three claims in the brief that did not survive checking

**1. "Free worker prose has no meaning field attached â€” so it passes anything."** Not true of this harness. I traced every path that puts a sentence into a composed prompt: a moving beat gets the library entry's motion text, a by-meaning stillness gets its entry's text, an ordinary rest beat gets a rotation entry's text, and the floor is a fixed constant. The worker never writes prose into a prompt â€” it names ids. And the knowledge pack carries a meaning list for all 307 entries it hands over (measured: 307 of 307 non-empty). The meaning was available all along. The real hole was simpler: the flat-hands rule was never *called* at the gate, and its wording demanded a word the refused prompt does not contain.

**2. "Spot 88 was exactly that failure."** Spot 88 was not a Prompter 2 prompt at all. Its sentence lives in the hand-authored prompt file written by an earlier session and fired by an older script. None of Prompter 2's own 52 designed prompts carries the shape.

**3. "Job 3609 is in the stored prompt history."** The prompt is; the praise is not. Job 3609 has no comment in the prompt-snapshot store â€” I checked all 160 comment events. The praise exists only in the learning-loop comment store. It is genuine ("amazing work with the handsâ€¦ the hands kind of touching the table meaning outlining the layoutâ€¦ that can be used much more"), recorded as praise with a gesture-locus, so the acceptance target stands â€” but the record it sits in is not the one the brief named.

The brief's *confirmed* claim: all three narrower escapes â€” "flatten onto the surface", "flat onto the wood", "press flat to the table" â€” really did slip past the old rule. Its "13 entries" figure I could not reproduce; the closest naive widening I could construct flags **11**.

## Task A â€” the meaning at the gate

Proved from the code, not assumed. Each beat's sentence is now tagged, as it is chosen, with the entry it came from. That mattered: the obvious shortcut â€” using the final id list â€” would have been wrong, because for an ordinary rest beat that list holds an id from one pool while the *sentence* comes from a different rotation. Attributing a sentence to the wrong entry's meanings is exactly the kind of quiet mismatch that makes a gate look like it is working.

The gate now calls the library's own rule per beat with that beat's meanings â€” the same function the vocabulary screen runs, not a second copy. Prose that belongs to no beat (header, mood, resting-hand line, closer) is screened with no meaning, which refuses rather than permits. Max's narration is blanked by the existing quoted-narration mask, called rather than reimplemented. A live dry run confirmed every beat resolved to an entry.

## Task B â€” the discriminator

**Why the wording alone cannot do it.** Spot 88 reads "her two hands come to rest a little apart on the tableâ€¦ then settle". Job 3609 reads "her hands press a little lower onto the table, settling with a slight weight, then hold". Both are both-hands, arriving at the table, staying. No wording rule can separate them. What separates them is that 3609's beat means *weight and stillness* and spot 88's means *comparison*.

So the wording layer's job is only to **see** both, and the meaning decides. Four things make it see them without accusing the innocent:

- **Both hands** â€” widened to the bare phrase "her hands", which the old rule did not know and which is exactly what the praised beat uses.
- **The word "flat" is no longer required.**
- **The placement must open the description.** A gesture that ends by putting the hands back is a return to rest â€” how most of this library ends. A gesture whose only motion is the hands arriving is the refused shape.
- **The hands must stay.** Touching the table and moving on is the use Max asked for much more of.

Bare "rest" and "stay" are deliberately *not* arrival verbs. Including them was tried and measured: 29 flags, nearly all of them furniture.

## Measured results

**Whole base, 322 non-banned entries:** 4 flag on wording; **1 is refused** after the meaning is applied.

| entry | meanings | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| palms_settle_down | acceptance, grounding, reassurance, stillness | passes |
| single_calm_exhale_hands | grounding, letting go, reassurance | passes |
| rest_hands_settle_a_little_lower | closing, grounding, stillness | passes |
| **release_both_hands_lower_and_come_to_rest_apart** | letting go, pointing out | **refused** |

Hand-checked all four. The refused one is the spot-88 shape verbatim â€” both hands lower onto the table a little apart, come to rest, stay still â€” carrying "pointing out". A genuine catch, not a false alarm.

All seven false-alarm shapes still pass, verified: hands already flat as furniture; palms flat against each other; hands on their little-finger edges; "the flat of" as a noun; a single hand returning to rest; a return to rest ending another gesture; touching the table and moving on.

**Acceptance:** spot 88 caught with its comparison meaning; job 3609 passes with its real meanings (weight, stillness), and the two swap verdicts when their meanings are swapped.

**Every stored prompt â€” 284 unique texts, 869 beats.** Five beats flag when screened blind, all five from the older hand-authored pipeline; none of Prompter 2's 52 designed prompts flags. Supplying the entry each authored beat corresponds to:

- job 3626 spot 88 (refused reel) â†’ **still refused.** Correct.
- job 3609 spot 79 (praised) â†’ passes
- job 3607 spot 77 (praised) â†’ passes
- job 3612 spot 82 (renderer glitch, gesture called good) â†’ passes

One refusal out of 869 beats, and it is the one Max refused.

## Negative controls

Eight sabotages, each run against the full suite, each restored. Every one failed exactly the check it should and nothing else: gate wiring removed â†’ spot 88 sails through; old narrow wording restored â†’ 4 checks fail; stay-requirement, prior-clause, sequencing and already-furniture guards each removed â†’ their own sensitivity check fails; meaning filter removed â†’ job 3609 is refused; narration mask removed â†’ Max's words get screened. Two guards catch nothing live today. That is stated in the code rather than hidden, and each is proved reachable by a written control â€” the same treatment the existing diminutive escape already gets.

## TLDR

DUR1 was the invented rule, not the post-stroke hold â€” Max dictated held poses in his own words, in capitals, and complained about holds only twice, both times about a hand left *reaching*. I narrowed DUR1 to that, reworded the hold teaching to match, and added a general guard so no future rule can ban holding outright. The stale H4 line in the worker's brief is gone â€” it now reads the enforced rule itself. I refused to author a `comparison` gesture: the proposed fill already exists in the library.

---

## TASK 1 â€” DUR1 vs the post-stroke hold

### Provenance: DUR1

DUR1 came from a distiller run on 2026-07-29, from **one** comment, job 3470, whose defect is recorded as a **renderer** fault. Max's actual words there:

&gt; "Okay, the movements are good but so excessive that it's just idiotic. Both moments were right but somehow they weren't implemented right. I think what should be done is to do the same movement but very briefly for a fraction of a second and kind of **come back to balanced position**. So the movements are fine, but holding it for a long time makes this way stronger, too strong."

The gesture he was watching was *"she reaches one open hand gently outward and a little toward her own heart"* â€” a reach held out. He never said "never hold a gesture pose". The distiller wrote that sentence. Same failure mode as H4's "temple and ear only".

He said the same thing once more, job 3663: *"Doing two times and holding is too strongâ€¦ it should be one touch and then kind of **come back to balance**, not overdo it."* Two complaints, one remedy, one word: **balance**.

### Provenance: the post-stroke hold

Also derived â€” not from Max at all. A session authored it on 2026-07-30 from the gesture-science literature, to answer the looping complaint after discovering both looping reels already contained an anti-loop prohibition. I checked the two citations: **Kita, van Gijn &amp; van der Hulst (1998)**, "Movement phases in signs and co-speech gestures", is real and defines the phases; **Flood &amp; Harrer (2023)**, "Kinetically-held questions: Representational gesture post-stroke holds in whole-class interactions in STEM", is real and is literally about teachers holding gestures. I did not independently confirm the specific 540 ms / 2.2â€“11.8 s figures. Notably, the standard model has a **retraction to rest after the hold**, which the base's teaching had dropped â€” so the literature was over-read in the same direction as DUR1 was over-read in the other.

### Which one Max actually said

**Neither, as written â€” but on the question "may a pose be held", the evidence is overwhelmingly his, and it is on the hold side.** His own dictated rulebook (2026-07-28) writes `balance_ball_hold` as *"HELD STILL â€¦ CRITICAL: this is a STATIC HOLD â€” the hands do NOT move or gesture while held."* And four review comments require a hold:

- 3469 â€” "You have to really **hold your hand still** as if you're touching the wall."
- 3478 â€” "It should be like **touch and hold** not kind of slide. Sliding is sensual, **holding is spiritual**."
- 3616 â€” "put two hands together a little lower and **hold it there**, trying to meditate."
- 3619 â€” "you **hold the hands steady, don't gesture** â€” and she is trying to gesture. **That's a mistake.**"

### The ruling (mine, not handed to Max)

Your hypothesis was right, and Max's own word makes it sharper than "aloft under tension". **The discriminator is position, not duration, and not height.** The hold belongs to the position she can *stay in* â€” resting on the table, landed on a place she touched, quietly together with the other hand, settled into a balanced shape. It never belongs to the position she is *reaching from*. A reach comes back to balance first, and the balanced position is what is then held.

DUR1 was over-stated, so I narrowed it and rewrote it around that discriminator, with all seven of his sources attached. I did not weaken it to fix a number: it still forbids exactly what he complained about, and it now says so in terms a worker can apply.

I also found the same over-reading running the *other* way and fixed it: the harness's own anti-loop sentence told the renderer that a hand "simply stays where it has come to" with no condition at all â€” which, on a reaching gesture, manufactures job 3470's defect on every long line. It now says the reach comes back to balance first. The worker's phase teaching carries the same distinction in the same words.

### The general guard

Rather than patch DUR1 by name, I added a **hold-contradiction guard**: it reads every live rule directive in the knowledge base plus every fixed piece of prompt text the harness writes, and reports any that forbids holding with no condition attached. The base *requires* holds, so an unconditional ban on them is a self-contradiction by construction, whoever writes the next one.

---

## TASK 2 â€” the stale H4 in the worker's brief

Confirmed: **both** branches of the worker's system text carried it, as hard rule 5 and hard rule 6. I deleted the copy rather than correcting it â€” the checklist now reads H4's own text out of the builder's rulebook, the same words the screens judge by. If the builder can't be read it falls back **closed** (hands off the face entirely), never to the retired sentence.

**Measured proof:** before, the harness-authored part of the brief named 2 of the 7 blessed sites. After, both branches name all seven â€” temple, ear, forehead, chin, nose, hair, lips â€” counted on word boundaries. Also verified independently: 7 of the 16 gestures Max approved are face touches and only 2 are temple or ear, exactly as you said.

---

## TASK 3 â€” `comparison`: I authored nothing, and the reason is a finding

I verified the research pass's sources. They hold up as *sources* â€” McNeill's abstract deixis, BÃ¼hler's *Deixis am Phantasma*, Gunter, Weinbrenner &amp; Holle (2015) for the N400/P600, Hinnell (2019) for the corpus, and the Cambridge observer study. But three things came apart on inspection:

1. **Every attested execution uses a form this base bans.** The ERP study's actress used "left hand to the left side and right hand to the right side"; Hinnell's corpus finding is explicitly that speakers "produce gestures with **alternate hands**"; the observer study used **palm-up open-hand** gestures, left-first and right-first. Alternating hands is H14 and Max's own standing rule; palm-up is H1. The single-right-hand one-finger form is an *inference* from the family, not an attested execution of it.

2. **The praise cited as cover doesn't cover it.** I read job 3609's actual prompt. It contains **no** layout-marking gesture â€” its four beats are a palm turning over, both hands folding inward, hands pressing a little lower, and one soft fade outward. Max's words ("outlining the layout on the table and showing the progression") describe what the *renderer* produced. That comment cannot certify any prompt wording as safe.

3. **The gesture already exists.** `two_points_touched_on_table` is live, offerable and reachable: *"Her right hand touches one place on the table and then travels slowly to a second place a little apart and touches there, one continuous motion across two distinct positions, then settles flat"* â€” meaning, *"Two separate points given two separate places, so they are felt as distinct."* Right hand, two loci, touching and moving on, one continuous motion so it cannot read as two. There are two further variants (`two_points_marked_hand_edge` in the air, `thread_connect_two_points` for a link). Writing abstract deixis would have been the eighth recorded instance of this base authoring a gesture it already owns.

On the demand: `comparison` is reached by 3 sentences and keeps 4 offerable entries â€” a *better* ratio than `layout`, which this base declined to fill on exactly that reasoning. The motivating sentence, "These are two different things", already reaches `separation`, a shelf with **19** offerable entries including all three variants above. I did not add a `comparison` link to `two_points_touched_on_table`: its meaning is distinctness, `comparison` is defined as "two options weighed side by side", and linking on the shape rather than the meaning is the rule I was told not to weaken.

One useful thing did check out: Max **did** say *"But finger is okay"* (job 3616), and his objection there was to the *meaning* a knocking finger carried, not to the finger. So the door isn't closed on a finger at the table â€” it just doesn't need a new entry walking through it.

# THE REGISTER INSTRUCTION

I had the sources verified independently. **Your research note was right about the thing that matters and wrong about four details**, all now corrected in what I wrote.

Right: **the shoulder-versus-elbow-and-wrist pivot is Efron's own analysis, quotable verbatim from a free primary source** â€” the 1937 preliminary paper, four years before the book: *"the Jew employing a relatively narrow or confined area, with the fulcrum at the elbowâ€¦ while the Italian sweep is characteristically large, with movements involving the entire arms and with the fulcrum at the shoulder."* Not a later summariser's gloss.

Wrong, and dropped: the "1,250 and 1,100" subject counts trace to a literary magazine and conflict with the Smithsonian's own finding aid ("some twenty-eight hundred"). Wrong, and corrected: his split is **traditional versus assimilated**, and he says explicitly it could *not* be explained by generation â€” "half recent immigrants, half assimilated second-generation" inverts his central finding. The "differed little in frequency" line is Graziano &amp; Gullberg's 2024 summary, not Efron's words. The Portuguese work is two *interactions*, not two people, and it is Africa-versus-Europe.

**And there is better evidence than the note knew about.** **MÃ¼ller 1998, Germans against Spaniards**: Spaniards produced more gestures *above shoulder height*, and **gesture frequency did not differ**. German is one of the three places Max put Anna, and this result **separates amplitude from rate** â€” Kita's conclusion is that the elevated lateral gesture creates an *illusion* of gesturing more often. **Kendon 2004b** has an English speaker who "moved the arm at the level of the waist" against a Neapolitan.

**The limits survive intact and are written into the doc**, not buried: Efron's pair is not Northern European; Kendon is one speaker against one; Cavicchio &amp; Kita ran 10 per group where their own power analysis wanted 22; the 12-Swede/12-Italian study coded rate and function only and never disclaims amplitude â€” it simply is not in the method; **no study anywhere measures gesture space for a Scandinavian group against a Southern European one**; and no study in this literature reports gesture size in physical units at all. It is written as a design instruction with its provenance and never as a fact about Northern Europeans.

**The instruction itself:** *her movements pivot at the elbow and the wrist, upper arm close to the body; they do not pivot at the shoulder, do not swing the arm out to the side, and do not rise to shoulder height or above.* Checkable by reading an entry, which "scale it down" never was â€” and it converges with H6 from the other direction.

# TLDR

The sweep is built and running: it reads 21,689 surfaces (3.43 million characters) and finds nothing live. Three of your claims were wrong, and one of the corrections uncovered a real hazard the brief did not know about â€” a **critical** rule protecting the learning loop was being silently deleted at every build. It is fixed, with the fix generalised so it cannot recur. 298 checks pass, production is untouched, nothing is committed.

---

## 1. What I built, and which surfaces it covers

Two files in the fork, data plus harness:

- **`gesture_kb\retired_readings_v01.json`** â€” the register. One entry per retired idea. It carries the reading's own subject vocabulary, probes, and the recorded decision about every surviving occurrence.
- **`gesture_kb\sweep_retired_readings.py`** â€” the sweeper. Runs with no arguments, exits non-zero if anything asserts a registered reading.

**It does not depend on a grammatical voice.** Voice is factored out and stated once as five reusable templates â€” declarative, imperative, definitional, caption, list item. A reading supplies only the words its subject is made of. That is what lets it see the imperative copy in L7 that the old guard was blind to.

**Surfaces, found by enumeration rather than by trusting your list:** every rule text and directive, gesture descriptions, meaning definitions, exemplar and counter-exemplar captions, stored prompts, source records, KB metadata, verdict prompts and comments; every source JSON the base is rebuilt from; every method document; string literals inside five Python files (extracted by parsing, not grepping, so comments and regex arguments are excluded); the prompter's module constants; and the worker's **rendered** system text and knowledge-pack brief.

Two things you did not ask for that turned out to matter. Text in this base is hard-wrapped at 78 columns, which had been splitting the foundational file's own sentence into a useless fragment â€” the sweeper now unwraps before splitting, and two probes contain literal newlines so removing that breaks the build. And any rule that announces its own correction must be registered or waived with a written reason, which is what makes the register self-extending: the next correction fails a check until somebody decides.

**Nothing is ever silently dropped.** An occurrence is either live (the sweep fails) or bucketed with a written reason printed on every run. Recorded decisions are pinned to the exact sentence, so editing that sentence re-arms them.

## 2. What it found over the three retired readings

- **"Temple and ear only" â€” NOT clean. Five surviving copies.** The research pack's own note justifying dropping a fetched gesture; Â§9 of the foundational instruction file; the KB method doc; the review-feedback method doc; and the origin sentence in the H07 findings document. Corrected or annotated in place, except Â§9 â€” see Â§8.
- **The unconditional hold ban â€” clean.** No surface asserts it. Two false positives, both scope statements about the *laced-finger* ban, recorded by name.
- **The symmetry ban â€” NOT clean.** Its ancestor was still alive in the lesson-1 rulebook's distilled reading of job 3607 and in the exemplar built from it, even though Max's verbatim beside it contains no prohibition at all.

I registered the DeepSeek claim as a fourth reading; it had 7 live surfaces including the rendered system text and brief.

## 3. What it provably cannot catch

It works sentence by sentence, so an idea split across two sentences is only found by an advisory pass that does not fail the build â€” that is exactly how the origin sentence hid. It can only look for readings somebody registered; it finds copies, never new inventions. Its vocabulary is deliberately narrow in places: "drop" had to be removed from the order-words because hands drop to the table in hundreds of legitimate descriptions, and that cost is written down rather than hidden. And it cannot see a retired idea that is paraphrased without any of its registered words.

## 4. The DeepSeek fix

Confirmed: the worker is Opus, and both `EN1` and the rulebook's `purpose` field told it DeepSeek orchestrates the spot. Corrected in two places in Max's dictated rulebook, plus one clause removed from a distilled reading. **Max's own dictated content is byte-identical** â€” his verbatim on job 3607 was not touched â€” and a new `corrections` array in the file records exactly what changed, what it was, and why. A separate method doc that genuinely does use DeepSeek got a header note saying it is not the current pipeline, and its DeepSeek sentences were left intact.

## 5. The eleven-rule verdict â€” three claims wrong

**It is not eleven, and they are not the right rules.** Measured:

- **7** rules mention "Extend *id*". Every target id exists as a rule row.
- Only **4** (CAM1, H12, L5, L8) have no separate base definition anywhere. All four restate their own content in full with their own evidence â€” nothing was lost, the wording is just self-referential. Your judgement was right for these.
- **7 ids were being silently replaced** at build time â€” and only 3 of them announced it. **R2, R3, R4 and R8 said nothing at all.** Reading the rules was not a way to find them, which is why the "Extend" phrasing was looking at the wrong set.

**One is a live hazard, not a legibility defect.** `X1`'s base carried, at priority *critical*, the finding that repetition is the dominant failure in Max's reviews, and the instruction that a repetition complaint must **never** be scored as a gesture-selection failure "because doing so would slowly train away exactly the gestures Max approves of". I searched all 69 live rules: **neither sentence survived anywhere.** The id was also quietly demoted from critical to high on the way through. That is a protection on the learning loop itself, deleted silently.

The other six lose nothing operative â€” their content is restated by H7, PR2, H6, I4, H9, R1, X6, L1, H4 or H1 â€” but that was luck, not design.

**So I extended the guard to all ids.** The earlier text becomes the head, the later text is appended and labelled, the stronger priority wins so a later distillation cannot demote a rule, and both source lists are kept. The hard-rule guard is byte-identical and untouched; hard ids are skipped so nothing is merged twice. Cost measured: the worker's pack grows from 172,756 to 180,747 characters, 4.6%.

**And `R1`:** the duplication is real, but "with a worse source list" is stale â€” R1's list was corrected on 2026-07-31 and is now identical to H9's, and R1's own text already says H9 carries the same ban. R1 is now a subordinate pointer: it no longer restates the ban, and keeps only what H9 lacks â€” where the ban is enforced, and the instruction for a composing worker. If H9 is ever narrowed, R1 needs no edit.

Standard three-phase packet route, per spot: emit the meaning-retrieval question, a fresh Opus reader answers it, emit the design packet with that answer, a fresh Opus worker answers it, ingest and gate and record. No flag was invented, no path improvised.

## The effect is real, and the brief's account of it is correct

I re-derived all four reels from the stored answers and the stored prompts.

In every one of the four, the director answered the bare word "stillness" and wrote a reason. In three of them the harness then wrote a movement into that beat:

- **Spot 9, line 2** â€” the director wrote that a hand moving there would be "motion without meaning" and that her hand should stay where it set the other voice down. The prompt that was built says her fingertips rest near the base of her throat and then lower back to the table. That is a rise and a return, and it is the second movement in a 7.3-second reel that was designed to hold one.
- **Spot 20, line 1** and **Spot 36, line 1** â€” the director wrote that her hands "decline it and stay where they are", and that moving would spend the reel's one motion on the setup. Both prompts say her hands lie together, open a little, then close.
- **Spot 38, line 1** â€” genuine stillness, as reported.

## The cause, exactly

The rotation that fills still beats holds sixteen entries. **Thirteen of them hold still. Three of them move** â€” the fingertips at the throat, and both of Max's rhythm poses. And the database already says so: those three are marked `reserved` in the intensity field, not `still`, by the sessions that authored them. The harness's own test for "is this a stillness" reads that same field and would have answered no for all three. It just was never asked, because the rotation handed entries out by arithmetic on the spot number, with no notion that a beat might have been left still on purpose.

**Why spot 38 differed is pure arithmetic, and nothing else.** The rhythm poses only fire when the spot number is divisible by four â€” 20 and 36 are, 9 and 38 are not. The remaining start index is the spot number times five, taken around a wheel of fourteen. Spot 9 landed on slot 3, which is the throat pose. Spot 38 landed on slot 8, which is "hands remain gently folded, unhurried, with no motion." Spot 38 came out right by luck. Given a different number it would have failed identically.

## Yes, the two kinds are distinguishable â€” but the proportion overturns the framing

The distinction is present in the stored data and was simply thrown away. A director's stillness is the bare word carrying a reason the worker wrote. The only other things that produce a still beat are a line the worker never covered, an unusable answer, or a gesture a gate or the duration cap removed â€” and each of those looks different in the record.

But the brief says an ordinary unchosen rest beat is the case the rotation legitimately exists for. **Measured over all 69 recorded designs: 158 rest beats were filled by the rotation, and 156 of them were the director's deliberate stillness. Two were not** â€” spot 48 line 5 and spot 98 line 5, both beats where a gesture was proposed and then removed. So the rotation is not mainly a frame-filler that occasionally lands on a chosen stillness; it is almost entirely a filler for chosen stillnesses. **Of those 156 deliberate beats, 33 received a moving entry.** The four reels last night were not an unlucky evening â€” they were the ordinary rate.

## What I changed

Only the harness, in one file, uncommitted. Nothing in the library was authored, edited, retired or unbanned; the throat pose and both rhythm poses are still in the rotation and still reach unchosen rest beats.

The harness now records, per beat, whether the director chose stillness with a reason. A beat that did is filled only from the thirteen rotation entries the data itself marks as holding still. A beat that did not is filled exactly as before, rhythm trick included â€” I checked that no unchosen slot changes anywhere in the 69 designs. Where no still entry is available, the beat falls to the one sentence that cannot fail, announced out loud; it can never fall back to a moving entry. The two rhythm rows do carry `stillness` while marked `reserved` â€” but `reserved` is not evidence that a row moves, so the two fields do not contradict the way the brief says. The real fault underneath was bigger and elsewhere: the meaning linker was making 39 links from words it had misread. Those are fixed at the derivation; the rows are untouched.**

## Three corrections to your brief

**1. "Two authored fields contradict each other" â€” the inference does not hold.** `intensity = reserved` cannot be read as "a movement." The original library, `gesture_library_v03.json`, holds 118 entries and exactly **one** of them says `still`; that value barely existed when it was written. Five rows whose own description says "remain completely still", "completely still", "motionless" sit at `reserved` â€” `waiting_still_pause`, `hand_rest_clasped`, `hand_rest_interlaced`, `med_knuckles_turn_out_warding`, `voice_hand_stays_where_the_voice_was_placed`. On those the *intensity* is the stale field and the stillness link is right. So no automatic rule can be built on the pair.

**2. It was never two rows.** 31 rows carried a primary `stillness` link while marked as something other than `still`. Two of them happened to be the rhythm entries.

**3. The harmful path was already closed, and the shelf has never been asked for.** Across all 69 stored designs the concept-reader has named `stillness` **zero** times, so no worker has ever been handed that shortlist. Every stillness ever chosen by meaning (4 occasions) was a genuine `intensity = still` entry. The rhythm rows have never been picked as a gesture in any design. And on the rotation lane the previous session's guard already refuses them on a deliberately-still beat.

## Rhythm-row verdict

Their `stillness` link comes from the words "comes to rest" in their own meaning sentence â€” the genuine repose sense, correctly matched. **I refused to change the rows and refused to strip the link**, because the only rule that would strip it would also strip it from the v03 poses that genuinely hold still. What I did instead: every build now prints the disagreement, split into "description names a motion â€” suspect the link" (21) and "description names no motion â€” suspect the intensity" (5), and resolves neither. It matters beyond tidiness: `stillness` is one of three meanings that **exempt an entry from the flat-hands rule H8**, so an accidental link switches a hard rule off silently. Measured today, exactly one entry's H8 exemption rests on a stillness link, and that one is legitimate.

## What I fixed â€” the derivation, not one row

The 2026-07-30 fix gave every keyword a **leading** word boundary and deliberately left the trailing side open, because some keywords are stems. That closed the front of the word and left the back open. A stem could run on into a continuation spelling a completely different word. Reading the build's own link audit: 545 of 993 links were made by a stem matching a longer word, and nearly all are correct inflections. **39 were not, and I read every one before touching it.**

| meaning | keyword | what it swallowed | links |
|---|---|---|---|
| listening | listen | listener / listeners â€” the audience noun, in nearly every use-when | 13 |
| evaluating | weigh | weight, weighty â€” which is the separate `weight` meaning | 8 |
| pointing out | point | "the point" as the essence or a moment, never a hand | 7 |
| stillness | rest | restraint, restricted, restating, restates; "the rest" as the remainder | 7 |
| closing | clos | closely, closer â€” the adverb of nearness | 2 |
| gathering | collect | collective | 1 |
| balance | even | event | 1 |

Two independent confirmations from the base's own instrumentation: its cross-named-keyword audit had already been flagging `evaluating`/"weigh" as naming another meaning, and that warning is now gone; and its universal-keyword audit had been reporting that "listen" matched 64 use-when fields, which is also gone.

## The number that matters

`setting aside` was not a vocabulary hole, and that is the finding. Its seven entries were already the right shape â€” one lateral move ending in a hold. Every one of them scopes that hold to **its own sentence**. Five of the shelf's nineteen sentences are short parallel refusals inside two spots ("Not force." / "Not performance." / "Not becoming something new."; "Not as excitement." / "Not as performance."), and four of those five are under the threshold at which a gesture has time to land **when measured one sentence at a time**. A worker handed seven one-sentence entries for a three-sentence run will fire one of them three times. That is the loop H7 forbids, arriving straight from the shelf.

Harrison 2010 (*Gesture* 10(1), 29â€“51) settles it: the stroke marks the **node** of a negation, the post-stroke hold marks its **scope**. One movement on the first item, stillness across the rest, turns 4.1 seconds of "impossible" slots into one motion with room to spare. That entry is the single most consequential thing in this pass.

## Measured before and after (ranked section)

| shelf | script | supply | pool | window | coverage |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| comparison | 5 | 4 â†’ **6** | 4 â†’ **6** | 1.8s | 0/5 â†’ **4/5** |
| setting aside | 19 | 7 â†’ **10** | 10 â†’ **13** | 2.0s | 0/19 â†’ **9/19** |
| explaining | 21 | 9 â†’ **11** | 16 â†’ **19** | 3.0s | 1/21 â†’ **9/21** |

`explaining` left the thinnest-twenty list entirely. `setting aside` fell from 7th thinnest to 15th. `comparison` still reads GENUINE HOLE at a pool of 6 â€” honest, and I did not pad it to move the label.

Every claim in the brief checked out: all three shelves carry a line cue, and the before-numbers matched exactly.

## Two more things the measurement overturned

**`comparison`'s disease is not thinness, it is a wrong family.** All four existing entries WEIGH â€” a two-pan balance, a poised midpoint, an uneven balance, a sweep that gathers. The narration never weighs anything; it names two items and then talks about each in turn. That is why spot 62 was offered `counting_both_hands` and refused it. The two new entries place and compare instead.

**Spot 10 is a wiring fault, not a hole, and I did not fix it.** "These are two different things." is the one `comparison` sentence still uncovered. A gesture meaning exactly that already exists â€” `aside_both_hands_part_a_little_and_stop`, whose own meaning line reads "These two were being taken for one thing, and they are not the same thing." It sits on `setting aside`, and none of that shelf's cues matches this sentence, so the sentence cannot reach it. The repair is a cue or a link. Authoring a near-duplicate to route around it would have made the base worse. Named and left.

Also: "Not force." shows as uncovered only because it is under the report's 15-character quoted-span floor. The entry is written for it.

## What was added â€” six entries, all candidates

Two on `comparison`, two on `setting aside`, two on `explaining` (one of which serves both). Every one links **only** to the shelf it was written for; the first draft produced eleven stray links (a mismatch entry landing on `connection`, a "not always" entry landing on `continuity`) and I rewrote the meaning text until the link audit was clean rather than leaving inversions in.

Best research find: in the Swedish Sign Language Lexicon, the signs for *therefore*, *cause/reason* and *explain* carry **identical** articulation descriptions, and *explain* differs by one trailing word â€” `upprepas`, repeated.
